# OWNER DECISIONS

Durable decisions already made. Do not silently overwrite; to change one, record the change here with date and who decided.

**Product principles**
- Open Stall is "GasBuddy for bathrooms": fast, simple, fun; nearest usable restroom.
- Public discovery works WITHOUT authentication. Accounts are for contribution and personalization.
- Accessibility is a first-class requirement. Low contributor friction.
- Preload useful data; keep honest verification, provenance and privacy. Pending submissions are never public. No user-uploaded photos in v1.
- Current UI is functional scaffolding. A full UI/UX/accessibility redesign comes after core functionality, data model and admin systems are stable (and before launch). No isolated polish before then.

**Contributions and abuse (2026-10-05/06)**
- A NEW restroom by a normal user must be submitted from their CURRENT location (device fix); normal users cannot place a restroom arbitrarily on a map. Admins/importers keep manual coordinate placement. Corrections to EXISTING restrooms do not require physical presence. GPS is an abuse-reduction signal, not proof. A public-place / not-private-home attestation is required.
- Nearby restroom proposals are NOT rejected or merged just for being close (malls, airports, parks, campuses, large stores, venues can hold several units). Likely duplicates are flagged privately for admin review; provenance preserved; hidden/pending info never exposed publicly. A near-identical repeat by the same account may be suppressed.
- Anti-abuse stays relatively LOOSE at first; thresholds (e.g. 3/hour, 5/day, caps, 50 m accuracy) are tunable starting values, not product assumptions (BACKLOG OS-208b). No invasive device fingerprinting; no paid anti-abuse services.

**Ratings and trust**
- Community/user ratings alone determine the public rating average and count. Admin ratings/observations stay as history/provenance and never affect the public rating/count; an admin-only place shows no community score.
- One public "Verified" concept; the backend retains whether the origin was admin or user. Repeat visits count as history/recency, not extra unique people. Public comments page deferred; private notes stay private.

**Data**
- Generic names ("Restroom") get enriched only from authoritative sources with provenance; never invent names (BACKLOG OS-308).
- Future (planning only, do not build): separate FACILITY/LOCATION from individual RESTROOM UNITS (BACKLOG OS-310). A single-room restroom with one toilet and one urinal behind one locking door is ONE unit.
- OSM-derived data stays subject to the ODbL gate (OS-110c) before larger imports or public launch.

**Process**
- Roles: Jake = owner/approval; ChatGPT = product + architecture oversight; Claude Code = primary builder (Sonnet unless approved otherwise). ChatGPT Work is the oversight/review/coordination layer in the normal development loop; GitHub and HANDOFF.md are the persistent shared communication layer. Routine implementation, testing, debugging and commits inside approved scope should not require Jake to relay messages.

### 2026-10-31 — P3 CORRECTED after Work's `f930574` (resumable exact recomputation, one request budget, hook work-loss fix); corrected delivery
- **Acknowledged:** your decision (keep the 5,000 starting budget; resumable exact recomputation for oversized chains; no 50,000 constant; no broad aggregates; narrowly scoped INTERNAL user-linked calculation state authorized) and both specific defects are valid. The accepted window-rule and rewind fixes and their coverage are preserved. Scope stayed inside `2528be6`'s boundary; P4 not started; R001-R006, migrations, app, package, env, CI and dependencies unchanged; no workflow or ref trigger.
- **SHAs:** corrected implementation `60e3eb4c8c730eeba28f8b9e1d13eebf803dbff8` (supersedes `e3f3bd0`); tests, harness, isolation, boundary and 109 of 110 mutants were executed at `328661f68fa76d77c68d54b316eadf2b6d38850c`, which differs from the implementation commit only by one stale mutant pattern in `mutations.tsv`, re-run at `60e3eb4`; evidence/docs `1611dcd12e59006b250f94b4c7bb7289d925b1fa`; this handoff commit follows. Superseded logs kept unchanged: `p3-*.681147d.txt`, `p3-*.e3f3bd0.txt`. Boundary baseline `2467aba`.
- **Actual strategy (concise):** (1) NEW private table `rewards.achievement_calc_state` (R007; internal, user-linked, writer-only, RLS forced, guard, at most 50 rows per account, purge DELETE only for the subject): one row per `(user, window)` holding a pass (`days` -> `locs` -> `done`), a (day, id) cursor with `last_day`, a (location, id) cursor with `last_loc`, and the running points/awards/days/locs. No set is stored: pass `days` walks the account's award rows in (day, id) order (points, awards, DISTINCT days by day transitions), pass `locs` walks them in (location, id) order (DISTINCT locations by location transitions), so exact distinct counts need O(1) state and no tradeoff beyond the operational-state allowance. A finished state holds the exact metrics. (2) Invalidation: `ach_run`, called by every real award/reversal/clearance hook, deletes the account's calculation states BEFORE its recoverable savepoint, so a recoverable failure cannot roll the invalidation back; relevant ledger changes only happen under the same user lock, so the check is atomic. Catalog changes need no invalidation (the state holds only metrics). (3) The evaluator advances a state by at most the remaining request budget per call, never exposes a partial number or event (unfinished chains return as `remaining`), and a probe that finds a fresh window over budget starts the state, so the next call steps instead of probing again. The `deferred` column and permanent deferral are REMOVED. (4) Only finite quiescent histories are claimed to complete: a relevant change restarts the calculation (stated limit).
- **Accounting defect fixed:** ONE logical request budget of 5,000 RAW award rows (standing or reversed) covers read-repair (`ach_process_queue(user, budget)` returns its scanned count), display, cache hits and misses and rejected probes; an over-budget probe is charged INCLUDING its sentinel (budget + 1) and cached; when the remaining budget is 0 no probe is made. `get_my_progress` reads a finished exact state for free. `evaluate_achievements` also charges its rejected probes (its returned `scanned` now includes them) and takes `p_budget`. The public repair batch is capped at 20,000 counted raw rows (documented, measured).
- **Work-loss defect fixed:** `ach_run` persists every unfinished chain (`remaining`) atomically after the recoverable savepoint, also when no queue row existed. `p3_12` runs the three REAL hooks on a 6,000-award account (visit, clearance, reversal-with-revoke): the oversized chain leaves a queue row where none existed, no partial number or event, progress reports unavailable (never the stale badge as earned), and repair alone (no further visit) completes the exact award (30,005 of 30,005) or the exact revoke (30,000 below 30,005, citing the reversal entry and its final decision) and cleans the queue.
- **Raw candidates, reversal probes and the work model:** the metric read and every calculation step bound RAW award rows with LIMIT (budget + 1 sentinel), not standing rows. Stated model, enforced by assertions that measure REAL work from `pg_stat_xact_user_tables` (heap tuples read plus index scans started), independent of the engine's own counter: per raw row at most one raw tuple, one reversal-probe scan and one probe heap tuple, i.e. at most 3 x 5,001 plus a few hundred per call. Measured: progress with fifty lifetime chains plus a second over-budget window on a 6,000-award account = 5,002 total (one charged probe), and 5,002 again with read-repair in the same request; adversarial account with 8,000 raw awards of which 7,900 are reversed (100 standing): completed EXACTLY (500 points, the reference days and locations) in four steps of at most 15,001 each (EXPLAIN of one step in `p3-bounds-explain.txt`); the repair batch over seven oversized accounts stopped at counter 20,004 / 24,004 measured and eventually completed all seven. The standalone-query EXPLAIN is labelled as a simplified query, and the self-reported `scanned` is no longer treated as proof.
- **Two production hazards found by these tests and fixed (not previously known):** with stale statistics an unpinned planner chose a bitmap scan + sort and a hash anti-join, reading the account's whole history and every reversal; and a `cursor is null or ...` predicate is not an index condition in a cached generic plan. The bounded reads now use sentinel bounds, pin ordered index scans with LIMIT and nested-loop probes (no bitmap, seq scan, sort, hash or merge join), custom plans, and `jit = off` (the disabled-sort cost otherwise triggered about 270 ms of JIT per call, caught by the wall-clock guard at 376 ms per user). New additive indexes: `(user, day, id)`, `(user, location, id)` and a reversal index for the revoke basis (a backward index scan with LIMIT 1 instead of scanning reversals).
- **Mutants (all killed; two survived my first test sets and were fixed by sharper assertions):** ignored unfinished output (`p3_run_persist_remaining_removed`), uncharged probes (`p3_progress_probe_uncharged`), repeated over-budget scans (`p3_budget_unlimited`, `p3_progress_budget_unlimited`), separate read-repair/display budget (`p3_progress_separate_budget`), missing invalidation (`p3_calc_invalidate_removed`), cursor not saved, days and locations double counted at chunk boundaries, partial state exposed, purge not deleting calculation state, batch cap removed, planner pin removed, repair slots consumed by skipped users, and the earlier window-rule/rewind/cursor/cache mutants.
- **Results (container PG16.15, Claude-run, env -i allowlist/temp HOME/socket-only):** 72 test-file runs, 0 failed: P1-only 10 files/306 ok lines; P2 8/281; P1-on-P2 9/261; legacy-on-P2 8; P3 13 files/344 (p3_01 42, p3_02 27, p3_03 31, p3_04 35, p3_05 33, p3_06 34, p3_07 30, p3_08 32, p3_09 29, p3_10 9, p3_11 6, p3_12 14, p3_13 22); earlier suites again on the P3 build 16/478; legacy-on-P3 8. Mutations 110 (24 P1 + 42 P2 + 44 P3): 108 killed, 2 documented equivalent (`lock_user_location_day`, `p2_visits_guard_nonidentity_check`), 0 survived, 0 invalid. Boundary selftest 17/17, check 20/20, `--against 2467aba` 24/24. Isolation 27 per invocation. The catalog inventory (`p3_04`) now has 17 tables, 61 policies, 46 rewards + 9 public functions and a 130-statement executed refusal matrix (22 permitted, 108 refused). Earlier-phase checks keep exact phase-aware expectations; nothing weakened or deleted.
- **NOT RUN / not claimed:** your independent PG16 execution (no validation ref; the pinned workflow is unchanged and I will not trigger one speculatively); `npm run check`; macOS/other PG versions; UI, VoiceOver/TalkBack or device behavior; hosted DB, production; a formal proof of interleavings; `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` as a bound on a running RPC; a real `pg_cancel_backend` during a calculation step (the step rolls back with its transaction, proven with a forced failure; hook-level real cancellation remains covered by `p3_07`); completion under a continuous stream of relevant changes (a calculation restarts on each relevant award or reversal; only finite quiescent histories are claimed to complete). ### 2026-10-31 — P3 CORRECTED after Work's CORRECTIONS_REQUESTED (`7c58095`); corrected delivery plus a measured technical proposal
- **Acknowledged:** all three findings and the test/evidence corrections are valid. Scope kept inside `2528be6`'s boundary; P4 not started; R001-R006, existing migrations, app, package, env, CI and dependencies unchanged; no workflow or ref trigger.
- **SHAs:** corrected implementation `e3f3bd0729763543c2cc17854a2a64dd3ace7d22` (supersedes `681147d`); ALL tests, harness, mutations, boundary and isolation were executed at exactly this commit; evidence/docs `5c26dccb078b025ea8181f849554bd03666b17e6`; this handoff commit follows. First-delivery logs are kept unchanged as `docs/evidence/rewards/p3-*.681147d.txt`; `p3-*.txt` are the corrected ones (each headed with its SHA). Boundary baseline `2467aba`.
- **Finding 1 (whole-call work bounds):** one engine call now reads at most 5,000 STANDING awards in total. A single budgeted pass per distinct window returns all four metrics (`rewards.ach_metrics`, cached per window inside the call, so fifty lifetime definitions cost one scan); a metric is NEVER partial. The evaluator stops when the next chain does not fit the rest of the budget and returns exactly that tail as `remaining` (resumed next call, dirty scope kept); a chain whose OWN window holds more than the whole budget is DEFERRED into `achievement_eval_queue.deferred` (new column, cap 500): no event, reported `unavailable` (no number), never retried in a loop (repair skips deferred-only rows). `get_my_progress` applies the same budget and cache. The full re-scan's cursor is now the last entry actually PROCESSED (a budget stop no longer skips the tail of a page). Candidate enumeration uses loose index probes (new partial index `reward_ledger_standing_day_idx`, an additive R007 object) instead of a scan of every award. Counted honestly: the budget counts standing awards read; rows of reversed awards (rare, each needs two admins) are probed through the unique reversal index and not counted.
- **Finding 2 (window rule per key):** `guard_achievement_definitions` refuses, with `23514`, a later version whose `window_rule` differs from the previous one (a different window type needs a separate logical key), so a future-dated version can no longer strand an existing chain. `p3_03` proves four refused incompatible inserts (none stored), unchanged rule/selection/scope/progress, a still-working evaluation of the existing lifetime chain, and that a compatible later version is still accepted. Mutant `p3_rule_invariant_removed` is killed.
- **Finding 3 (overflow rewind):** `ach_enqueue` now REWINDS the re-scan cursor whenever a new union is truncated at 500. `p3_10` is the real fixture: a re-scan page advanced to `ox10|2026-07`; a late 2025-11 award fails its hook (recoverable) and is queued; 600 more valid entries arrive and the cap drops the 2025-11 entries; the cursor is reset, the bounded drain finishes, all ten 2025-11 chains are awarded and the row is deleted only after the full pass. Mutant `p3_queue_rewind_removed` is killed. `p3_11` separately proves a re-scan page that stops on the budget resumes exactly at the first unprocessed entry (mutant `p3_cursor_page_end` killed).
- **Test and evidence corrections:** the `OR TRUE` assertion in `p3_08` is replaced by an exact check (the remaining 70 are exactly entries 51..120 in order, and nothing was evaluated early). `p3_09` labels now say what is measured: the EXPLAIN is of a SIMPLIFIED standalone query, the "109-chain" timing is a 109-chain timing, and the whole-call claims rest on the evaluator's own `scanned` counter: 50 lifetime keys on a 3,285-award user = ONE call, scanned 3,285; 36 monthly windows + the lifetime chain = scanned 3,285 and exactly `["b_pts|lifetime"]` returned; a 519-chain drain through 500 dirty entries, overflow and the paged re-scan takes 21 steps and ends in the EXACT reference state for every chain (computed from the ledger); a 6,000-award user's lifetime chain is deferred (no event, no partial number, repair does not loop, progress unavailable) while its window chains stay exact; progress itself refuses an over-budget number. New budget/cache/deferral/cursor mutants: `p3_budget_unlimited`, `p3_metrics_cache_removed`, `p3_deferred_dropped`, `p3_cursor_page_end`, `p3_repair_spins_on_deferred`, `p3_progress_budget_unlimited`, `p3_chain_bound_unbounded` (all killed; two survived my first test set and were fixed by adding assertions, not by excusing them).
- **Results (container PG16.15, Claude-run, env -i allowlist/temp HOME/socket-only):** 70 test-file runs, 0 failed: P1-only 10 files/306 ok lines; P2 8/281; P1-on-P2 9/261; legacy-on-P2 8; P3 11 files/296 (p3_01 34, p3_02 27, p3_03 31, p3_04 35, p3_05 31, p3_06 34, p3_07 30, p3_08 32, p3_09 27, p3_10 9, p3_11 6); earlier suites again on the P3 build 16/478; legacy-on-P3 8. Mutations 101 (24 P1 + 42 P2 + 35 P3): 99 killed, 2 documented equivalent (`lock_user_location_day`, `p2_visits_guard_nonidentity_check`), 0 survived, 0 invalid. Boundary selftest 17/17, check 20/20, `--against 2467aba` 24/24. Isolation 27 per invocation. One more earlier-phase check (the P2 retention-interval scan) lists the three window-arithmetic functions exactly; nothing weakened or deleted.
- **Measured technical proposal for Work (routine technical scope, not an owner gate):** with the budget at 5,000, a chain whose OWN history exceeds it (for example a lifetime-wide chain for a user with more than 5,000 standing awards) stays pending and is reported `unavailable`; it is exact for everyone below the line and fails closed above it. Measured cost of an UNBUDGETED exact lifetime read on this runner: 3.1 ms for 3,285 standing awards and 4.8 ms for 6,000, roughly 1 ms per 1,000 awards (a window read is an index range). Option A (recommended): raise the per-call budget constant to about 50,000 awards (about 50 ms worst case, still one cached pass per window), keeping the same deferral for anything beyond it; no schema, privilege, retention or authority change. Option B (only if real users can approach tens of thousands of awards): per-user incremental aggregates maintained by the visit, clearance, reversal and purge paths (O(1) lifetime metrics); this adds a user-linked table, backfill, reconciliation, purge/severing rules, new guards and privileges, so it is an architectural tradeoff I will not start without your decision. Until you choose, the delivered behavior is fail-closed and exact.

- **What it does:** immutable versioned definitions (insert only by the migration owner/tests; NOTHING seeded), account-scoped opaque chains, append-only award/revoke events (revoke cites the lifetime reversal ledger entry and its P2 `reward_decisions` row), a repair queue. - **Acceptance criteria map:** (1) worked cases incl. last/non-last reversal, same-date locations, threshold raise/lower, late clearance, revoke/re-earn/replay, DST/zone/season: `p3_02`, `p3_03`. (2) failure classes: `p3_06` (OSA01 on visit, clearance, reversal preserves everything and commits a dirty queue; 42501/23505/XX000/OSA02/40001/40P01/RW040 abort the transaction) and `p3_07` (a REAL held chain lock gives 55P03 within the bounded wait, visit and both rewards commit; a REAL statement_timeout and a REAL `pg_cancel_backend` raise 57014 and leave nothing). (3) lock order/races: `p3_07` repair vs reversal, clearance and account deletion, a waiting reversal behind a purge (RW030, no 40P01), two concurrent repairs, fair ordering (a locked front user does not use up the call's slots), plus the P2 global-day/month race tests re-run on the P3 build (and killed as mutants there). (4) bounds: `p3_08` (50 chains per evaluation, union/cap 500/overflow flag, retained backlog, bounded full re-scan, 50-per-page progress) and `p3_09` EXPLAIN evidence: 3,285-row user read 2.7 ms index scan, 50 chains 41 ms, 2,000 users x 4 chains 1.7 s, 109-chain backlog 67 ms; far inside the 5,000-row/2 s hypothesis (seed used trigger-disabled bulk inserts for the SEED ONLY). (5) public RPCs: `p3_06`, `p3_08` (28000, 22023 cursor/size/limit, anon/service_role refused, disabled/unset returns no numbers, never earned for an ineligible badge, pending reported unavailable not zero). (6) purge: `p3_05` (two accounts sharing key and period, severing, repeated purge a no-op, deleted subject recreates nothing, all guards). (7) complete inventory and a 105-statement executed refusal matrix: `p3_04`; 27 P3 mutants.
- **Decisions inside the released boundary, flagged for your review:** (a) chains are serialized by per-chain advisory locks (not `FOR UPDATE`), so the writer holds no UPDATE on chains; the 55P03 path is a real bounded advisory-lock wait. Retained and deleted rows are two disjoint partitions; only the deleted partition is aggregated (worked example A1-A4 in the plan becomes a test fixture).

**Corrections to Work's review at `fe8c81e` (2026-10-07) — implemented locally in commit `4b525a1`; nothing applied live**
1. Moderated evidence is preserved. `delete_my_account` now deletes only genuinely unmoderated contributions (pending submissions with NO moderation decision, open reports). Held, released and decided submissions keep their original and their full append-only decisions with `user_id` severed; the moderation log is untouched. `withdraw_my_submission` is likewise limited to never-moderated items (a released item with hold history cannot be withdrawn). The `open_stall.allow_provenance_purge` bypass is removed entirely: `reject_append_only_change` now always raises, so update/delete/truncate on `moderation_decisions` and `moderation_log` are impossible. Source migrations `20261009000001` and `20261009000002` were edited in place (neither has been applied live, so no new migration was needed). Tests changed: a held submission now survives account deletion with hold/release/hold history intact and the submitter severed; unmoderated pending items are still removed; released-with-history cannot be withdrawn; plain pending can.
2. Two-session concurrency tests added to `supabase/tests/concurrency.sh` (run by `npm run test:db`): real competing transactions for two administrators on one submission — approve/approve, approve/reject, reject/approve. The first transaction holds its row lock open for 2 s while the second arrives; the second fails with "already decided", leaving exactly one final decision, at most one public location, and no location for a rejected submission. Removing the `FOR UPDATE` lock makes the suite fail (mutation checked).
3. Text-only edit-and-approve is now explicit in the admin UI ("text fields only; yes/no facts approve as proposed; reject or hold if they look wrong") and in ADMIN_REVIEW.md. Admins can reject or hold inaccurate boolean proposals. Keeping the stricter no-self hold/release rule and the owner-SQL first-admin bootstrap, per Work's answers. First-admin insertion remains a live-mutation approval gate.

Mutation check: deleting without the "no decision" filter fails the suite.

**Phase 3A migration approval — dry-run attempt (2026-10-07): STOPPED BEFORE APPLYING; nothing applied**
- Authorization received from Jake/Work: preview first; apply \`20261009000001_admin_identity\` and \`20261009000002_moderation_core\` ONLY if a true dry-run confirms exactly those two; if the connected tooling cannot do a true dry-run, stop and report. I am following that condition literally.

The CLI's \`supabase db push --dry-run\` is the real preview, but this container has no Supabase CLI login/link.
- Equivalent evidence I CAN show without any mutation (this is what \`db push --dry-run\` computes, but it is NOT the tool's own dry-run, so I did not treat it as satisfying the gate): remote ledger via \`list_migrations\` = exactly 10 entries, 20261004000001 … 20261008000001, unchanged since the earlier review; repo \`supabase/migrations\` = those same 10 plus exactly two newer files, \`20261009000001_admin_identity.sql\` (sha256 0e7f2d07…) and \`20261009000002_moderation_core.sql\` (sha256 66ab8e50…). So the pending set is exactly the two approved migrations.
- **Additional blocker found for applying through the MCP (independent of the dry-run question):** \`apply_migration\` takes only \`name\` and \`query\`; it cannot set the migration version, so the ledger would record a server-generated timestamp version instead of \`20261009000001\`/\`20261009000002\`. Every earlier ledger entry matches the repo filenames exactly (applied by the CLI). Applying via the MCP would desync the ledger from the repo: a later \`supabase db push\` would see the repo versions as unapplied and try to re-run them (failing on already-existing objects). Repairing that needs \`supabase migration repair\` via the CLI. I therefore recommend the CLI path (\`npx supabase db push --dry-run\` then \`npx supabase db push\` from Jake's linked Mac) for the actual apply, not the MCP.
- Options for Jake/ChatGPT (any of these needs a recorded decision): (A) Jake runs \`npx supabase db push --dry-run\` on the Mac, confirms exactly the two migrations, then applies with \`npx supabase db push\`; Claude then runs the post-migration read-only verification through the MCP (ledger, tables/functions/grants, advisors, new-warning comparison) and records it. (C) Accept the ledger comparison above as the "preview" AND approve the MCP route (still carries the version mismatch).

**What changed**

Reads/writes are `admin_*` RPCs with the public anon key. `proxy.ts` refreshes the session on /review too.

The ENVIRONMENT.md project-ref guard (`assertSessionBackend`) is kept. Hosting is still NOT approved.
- **New forward migration (NOT applied live):** `supabase/migrations/20261010000001_admin_seed_review.sql`: `admin_location_counts()`, `admin_list_locations(p_status, p_limit)`, `admin_get_location(p_id)`, `admin_apply_location_review(...)`, internal `admin_location_json(uuid)`. All SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=''`, `authenticated`-only (anon denied). Each calls `require_admin()` (admin_users + aal2). The write wraps `apply_location_review_v2` unchanged (verification semantics, `open_stall` source row, visit details, community-only public ratings), records reviewer identity `admin:<auth uid>`, appends a `seed_review` row to the append-only `moderation_log`, and refuses (42501) a restroom the caller contributed (self-adjudication). Legacy `local-admin` rows are untouched; `apply_location_review(_v2)` remain service_role-only for importer/manual tooling.
- Safety of a not-yet-applied migration: until the owner applies it, `/review` shows "The required review database update is not installed yet. Nothing was saved." (list pages show a friendly load error); `/queue` is unaffected. `npm run doctor` treats the four new functions as INFO "not installed yet" when missing and as a failure only if anon can call them.
- Small UX/accessibility: view tabs are labelled links; Username helper text now says history is tied to the signed-in admin; link between /review and /queue; form controls verified labelled and keyboard-operable.
- Docs reconciled: ADMIN_REVIEW, ARCHITECTURE (admin description no longer says service_role/local-only), SECURITY, DATABASE, TESTING, BACKLOG (OS-301b; OS-209 "cascades all account data" corrected to match implemented preservation of moderated evidence), PROJECT_STATE (stale "Phase 3 not started"/"awaiting review" header fixed), `.env.example`. Dated historical evidence retained.

**Files:** apps/admin/src/{app/page.tsx, app/review/{page,actions,ReviewForm,[id]/page}.tsx, lib/{queue,requireAdmin,gate,gate.test,moderation,moderation.test}.ts, proxy.ts}, lib/db.ts (deleted), scripts/{e2e-admin.mjs, doctor-live.mjs}, supabase/migrations/20261010000001_admin_seed_review.sql, supabase/tests/seed_review_admin.test.sql, plus the docs above.

**Exact tests and results (all run locally in this container)**

## Harness correction delivered (response to Work's review 5306f99) — 2026-10-08 (Claude)

**Commit:** `a914822328a91be821bb31736bfcd5b9274f20a6` (harness only: `scripts/e2e-admin.mjs`). No product, migration or test-SQL change; migration `20261010000001` remains NOT applied live; no production contact.

**What changed in `scripts/e2e-admin.mjs`:** mock listen, dev-server spawn, readiness wait and browser launch now all happen inside the single `try/catch/finally` that runs the tests, so any setup failure is reported and cleaned up.
- Readiness: each request has `AbortSignal.timeout(3000)`; total startup is bounded (`E2E_ADMIN_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 120000); the loop now **fails explicitly** if readiness is not reached, and also fails immediately if the dev server exits early or cannot spawn.
- Browser launch is wrapped in a 60 s timeout; mock `listen` errors (e.g. EADDRINUSE) reject instead of crashing.
- `cleanup()` is idempotent and stops only what this run started: its browser (10 s bound), its own detached server **process group** (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5 s), and its mock (only if it started it, so it never closes someone else's listener). SIGINT/SIGTERM run it too.

**Failure-path tests run locally (intentional faults), each finite with exit code 1, a clear message, and afterwards no leftover next/chromium processes and ports 3199/54299 free (checked with /dev/tcp; an earlier `ss` check was invalid because `ss` is not installed here, so I redid it properly):**
- A) `E2E_ADMIN_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS=1` -> "dev server was not ready within 1 ms", 2 s.
- B) `CHROME_BIN=/bin/true` (real browser-launch failure) -> "browserType.launch: ... closed", 41 s.
- C) app port 3199 occupied by another listener -> "dev server exited before it became ready", 2 s.
- D) mock port 54299 occupied -> "listen EADDRINUSE", 1 s; the foreign listener was left alone (I killed my own blocker processes afterwards).

**Sequential verification after the change (no concurrent `.next` writers):** `npm run check` (lint, typecheck of all workspaces incl. `next typegen`, unit tests admin 12 / 32 / 105 / 65 / 15, secrets scan 217 files) exit 0; then `node scripts/e2e-admin.mjs`: exit 0, 41 PASS, 0 FAIL ("Admin e2e passed"); then `npm run build -w @open-stall/admin`: compiled, `/review` and `/review/[id]` present. I did not re-run `npm run test:db` for this commit because only the Node harness changed (the SQL suite and migration are byte-identical to 20cdb67, where it passed; Work reproduced it).

**Limitations:** unchanged from the delivery entry (local Postgres with simulated claims; mock backend; no real-MFA `/review` session). Cleanup is Linux/macOS-oriented (`process.kill(-pid)`).

**Next action:** Work completes its independent validation and decides acceptance. Claude idle at :30 checks.

## Delivery: R1 public discovery vertical slice + shared design foundation — 2026-10-09 (Claude)

**Implementation commit:** `0b3aa1281c836339fa60c3c7bd5d77062917a3c5` (local only; nothing deployed, no live DB/migration/mutation, no costs, no new provider/geography/feature; backend contracts, ranking, queries, caching and security untouched). Maverick correction and Holiday Inn report untouched. R2 NOT started (awaits Work acceptance of R1).

**Design direction (recorded in DESIGN_SYSTEM.md):** calm high-contrast utility; answer first (nearest usable restroom, distance, walk time, can I use it); one column on phones, list + map side by side from 900 px (max content width 1120); every state is a plain-language banner with one next step; discovery never asks for an account.

**What changed**
- **Shared foundation (`packages/ui`)**: `breakpoints`/`layout`/`layoutFor`, `focusRing` (3 dp outline, strong blue, offset 2), `tones` (info/success/warning/danger text+bg + symbol). Extra AA contrast pairs now enforced (muted-on-muted, status on plain surface, every tone, focus ring >= 3:1).
- **Shared components (`apps/mobile/src/components`)**: `StatusBanner`, `SegmentedControl`, redesigned result cards in `LocationList` (name heading, prominent distance + walk time, badge, only-known facts, community rating, "Nearest" label; list/listitem + link semantics), `FilterPanel` (disclosure with expanded state, always-visible removable active-filter chips, Clear all), `Screen` (max width, h1 + subtitle), `focus.ts` (`useFocusStyle` visible keyboard focus; `spaceActivates` Space on radios/checkboxes). Chip/PrimaryButton/SecondaryButton gained visible focus.
- **Discovery home (`app/index.tsx`, `LocationNotice`)**: one location prompt (the old home showed two near-duplicate buttons in the first state), then results header (live-region summary), nearest-verified hint, filters, banners (locating, denied with browser/OS steps, unavailable, offline saved results, service error + Try again, loading, no-match with Clear all filters, nothing nearby with Check again), List | Map switch on phones (List default) and side-by-side list + sticky map from 900 px. Same `useNearbyLocations` ranking/refresh and map abstraction/provider; no tile service change.
- **Domain helpers (tested)**: `describeActiveFilters`, `clearFilter`, `resultsSummary`, `quickFacts`, `communityRatingText`.
- **Defect found and fixed (affects the whole app, incl. R2/R3 screens):** react-native-web does not translate `accessibilityState`, so Chip/toggle/button state (checked, selected, expanded, disabled) was NOT exposed on the web. Shared components now use `aria-checked`/`aria-selected`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-disabled`. Radios/checkboxes also now activate with Space (previously Enter only on web).

**Tests and results (all run locally in this container, sequentially)**
- `npm run check` (eslint, typecheck all workspaces, unit tests admin 12 / mobile 32 / domain 111 / importer 65 / ui 27, secret scan 228 files): exit 0.
- New `scripts/e2e-discovery.mjs` (`npm run test:e2e:discovery`, CI step added): builds the real Expo web export and drives headless Chromium against a local mock REST backend; fresh run **59 passed, 0 failed**. Coverage: single location prompt and one h1, keyboard-only start (Tab/Enter) with visible focus outline, results ranked nearest first, Verified/Unverified text badges, community-labeled ratings, only-known facts, one meaningful spoken name per card, List/Map switch by keyboard with a labeled map region, navigation to detail with no coordinates in the URL, filters (disclosure state, Verified only, accessibility filter, removable chips while collapsed, filtered-empty + Clear all), wide layout (map beside list, no switch), 320 px reflow without horizontal scroll, control audit (every control named, >= 48 px tall, exactly one h1), location denied + recovery, service error + recovery, empty area, offline saved results, privacy (only the public anon key, no user token; only map tile/font hosts attempted, all aborted).
- Existing browser suites re-run after the changes: `npm run test:e2e` (smoke) passed; `node scripts/e2e-auth.mjs` passed after one expected-copy update (the empty-state wording now lives in a banner title: "No restrooms found nearby yet", no trailing period).
- Evidence screenshots (Chromium, emulated 390x844 phone and 1280x800 desktop, mock data): `docs/evidence/r1/` — narrow start, list, map, filters open, denied, empty; wide discovery. Map tiles are not rendered in tests (network blocked), only markers.

**Honest limits**
- Chromium only (no Safari/Firefox); emulated viewports, not physical devices; no screen-reader run (semantics verified by DOM/role/name/state checks, not by VoiceOver/TalkBack/NVDA); native iOS/Android UI was type-checked and shares components but NOT run on a simulator or device (only the web export was exercised).
- No touch-target or contrast test of the map's own Leaflet controls or tiles (third-party UI); map markers still differ by color plus the label text.
- Detail, contribution, report, account, favorites and settings screens only inherit the improved shared components (focus, state exposure, Screen width); their own redesign is R2/R3. `LocationList` is shared with Favorites, which now also shows the card layout.
- Text scaling beyond browser zoom was approximated by the 320 px reflow check; OS-level font scaling was not tested.

**Remaining exact work for the milestone:** R2 (detail + evidence/reporting), R3 (accounts, favorites, submission/correction), R4 (integrated release-readiness validation). Not started.

**Next action:** Work reviews R1 and, on acceptance, issues R2 (or requests targeted corrections). Claude idle at :30 checks until then.

## Delivery: R3 accounts/favorites/settings/submissions/corrections + R2 provenance correction — 2026-10-09 (Claude)

**Implementation commit:** `5849fdedc042b249b457e6199467921f944e776e` (local only; nothing deployed; no live DB/migration/probe; no backend, RPC, validation, cap, rate-limit, GPS/attestation or security change; no new feature or provider; Maverick and Holiday Inn untouched).

If something here is out of date, tell us." and shows attribution only when present. The hours-source caveat in `hoursLabel` (for actual source hours text) is unchanged. I also removed the same blanket claim from `UNVERIFIED_EXPLANATION` ("It comes from public sources") since it is the same defect class; it now reads "Access and condition may differ from what is listed." Regression (facts.test.ts): without attribution no provenance line or the unverified explanation matches /public source|openstreetmap|from .* sources/, the last note is the source-neutral line, attribution is appended only when present.

**What changed**
- **Shared:** `Screen form` (640 px column on wide screens, `layout.formMaxWidth`), `TextField error` (`aria-invalid` + message under the field), reuse of `Section`/`StatusBanner`.
- **Auth** (`auth/sign-in`, `reset`, `callback`, `RequireAuth`): banners for errors/confirmations (generic wording unchanged), synchronous lock against double submit, network failure caught and explained with the form kept; "Almost done" / "Check your inbox" confirmations; reset-without-link and failed-callback states give a next step. Safe `next` handling untouched (`safeNextPath`).
- **Account:** sections (Your account, Add a missing restroom, Delete account), success banner after deletion, deletion lock; delete flow semantics unchanged (type DELETE).
- **Favorites:** loading/error (Try again)/empty (with "Find nearby restrooms")/full ("5 of 5 saved" + how to make room) states; distances still computed on device (no coordinates sent).
- **Settings:** sections, "Saved." confirmation for preference changes, inline display-name error, save lock, signed-out note says settings are saved on this device.
- **Contribute (new + correction):** grouped sections (About the place, Access and facilities, Location, Before you send), "Not sure" explained as unknown, location status banner, errors in one banner next to the submit button, attestation is a normal checkbox (no longer inside an alert), whole-submit lock (including the fresh-fix lookup), success banner with onward links, input kept on any failure. New restroom still needs a fresh current-device fix + public-place attestation and has no way to type/pick a position; corrections still need no location.

**Tests (fresh, one at a time):** domain 116, ui 28 (new form-width token test), `npm run check` exit 0 (lint, typecheck, all suites, secret scan); new `npm run test:e2e:accounts` (`scripts/e2e-accounts.mjs`, CI step added) **70 passed, 0 failed** (3 consecutive clean runs after one unreproduced click timeout on the first run, which I attributed to a test race: the favorites list can reload itself while the test is clicking "Try again"; the test now tolerates that). Also fresh: `test:e2e:detail` 72, `test:e2e:discovery` 75, `test:e2e:auth` passed (existing account journeys unchanged), `test:e2e` smoke passed. The accounts suite covers: sign-in failure/network failure/double activation, sign-up (weak password rejected before any request, one request on double tap), reset generic message, three hostile `next` values staying in-app and a safe one honored, favorites empty/error+retry/full/cap-message, settings and display-name rejection with the value kept, new-restroom submission (name+attestation required, caps and rate limits explained with the form kept, one proposal on double tap, device fix + accuracy sent as separate arguments, no coordinates in the payload, refused without location, no coordinate inputs), correction without any location (one request, pending-correction refusal explained, answers kept), account deletion (disabled until DELETE, failed deletion keeps the account, one delete on double tap, signed out afterwards), forms staying <= 640 px on wide screens, no horizontal scroll at 320 px, one screen-reader-reachable h1 per screen and labeled inputs, public reads never carrying the user token, favorites never carrying coordinates, no non-local request. **Mutation check:** removing the in-flight locks in sign-in, settings, contribution and deletion fails 6 same-tick double-activation checks (64 passed / 6 failed), restored afterwards.

**Finding worth knowing (not a defect introduced here):** the tab navigator keeps visited screens mounted but `aria-hidden`, so a naive DOM count shows two h1s after client-side navigation; screen readers only reach one. The new tests count only headings outside `aria-hidden` screens. R2's detail test counts on a fresh page load, where this does not arise.

## Delivery: R3 uncertain-outcome correction (response to Work's R3 review, 02fb9ec) — 2026-10-09 (Claude)

**Correction commit:** `af1c5bec8216555d39f00a7911a1d909faf1eb2c` (local only; nothing deployed; no live DB/migration/probe; no backend, RPC, idempotency or validation change; no new feature).

Acknowledged: R2 copy item closed by Work; R3 "Nothing was sent / Nothing was deleted" copy was false assurance for transport exceptions, and the deletion catch also spanned local sign-out after a confirmed deletion. Fixed as follows.

- **Classification:** `isUncertainOutcome(err)` (domain): a failed write is *certain* only when it carries a structured server `code` (SQLSTATE/PGRST: caps 53400, rate limit 54000, validation 22023, auth 28000/PGRST301 ...). Anything else (dropped connection, lost response, gateway error, thrown exception) is *uncertain*. `Outcome` failures now carry `uncertain`.
- **Copy:** uncertain writes show a warning "Not confirmed" notice: "We couldn’t confirm whether <your report was sent | your restroom was sent for review | your suggestion was sent for review | your rating was saved/removed | your favorite was saved/removed | your check-in was recorded>. We never resend automatically, so check first and then try again if needed." (`uncertainWriteMessage`), typed input is kept, and nothing is retried automatically. Confirmed rejections keep their specific messages (caps, rate limit, pending correction, validation, expired sign-in).
- **Deletion:** a lost deletion response says "We couldn’t confirm whether your account was deleted. Sign out and try signing in again to check..." and keeps the delete flow available. After a CONFIRMED deletion the success state is retained (the signed-in tools are hidden); if clearing the local session then fails, a separate warning "This device is still signed in" offers "Sign out of this device" (retry sends no second deletion request). The local clean-up exception is no longer folded into the deletion result.
- **Inspected all other catches I added:** sign-in/reset catches make no claim about what happened (reset now says it couldn’t confirm the new password was saved); LocationActions/report/contribute/account use the uncertain copy. Read/list failures keep their plain messages.
- **Also fixed while testing:** if the saved-state check on a restroom fails, the Save button used to stay disabled with no explanation; it now shows "We couldn’t check your favorites" with "Check again".

**Tests (fresh, one at a time):** domain 119 (isUncertainOutcome classification, copy never claims "nothing happened"), mobile 33 (api marks lost responses/throws uncertain and coded rejections certain), ui 28, `npm run check` exit 0; `test:e2e:accounts` **91 passed, 0 failed** (six consecutive fresh-build runs clean; earlier intermittent failures were test races: an injected error consumed by the page's own initial favorites load, now waited out); `test:e2e:detail` **83 passed**; discovery 75, auth, smoke pass. New LOCAL mock tests: the mock RECORDS the mutation and then destroys the socket (responses sent with `connection: close` so the browser cannot silently replay the POST) for rating, favorite, report, new restroom, correction and account deletion: asserted that the page says "couldn’t confirm", never says nothing was sent/saved/deleted, made exactly one request after waiting (no automatic duplicate), kept the typed input, and (new restroom) the mock really did record the item. Confirmed deletion followed by local clean-up failure (storage removal made to throw): success state retained, device banner shown, no "nothing was deleted", delete action gone, retry signs out with deleteCalls still 1. **Mutation check:** reverting the uncertain handling (contribute copy, api classification, deletion state) fails 9 checks; an earlier version of the confirmed-deletion test was vacuous (the auth client signs out locally even when the logout request fails, so the "device still signed in" banner appeared while the app was already signed out). That exposed a false banner; the device warning now only shows while the app is actually still signed in, and the test forces the failure through storage.

**Honest limits:** Chromium only, emulated viewports, no screen reader; native not run; the real auth client's behavior when local session removal fails was only emulated through browser storage; no real backend. Screenshots: `docs/evidence/r3/narrow-5-uncertain-outcome.png`, `narrow-6-deleted-cleanup.png` (and the earlier R3 set).

## Delivery: R4 integrated validation + final uncertainty corrections — 2026-10-09 (Claude)

**Commits:** uncertainty corrections `d3d625c`; integrated validation, fixes and docs `5b9a6e0b778a9b8abee20d5aa151727ef0e82b77`. Local only; nothing deployed; no live DB/migration/probe; no backend, RPC, data, ranking or security change; no paid tooling; no new feature; Maverick and Holiday Inn untouched.

**1. Final uncertainty corrections (Work's two items)**
- `UNCERTAIN_DELETE_MESSAGE` no longer concludes "if not, it was deleted". It now says not to assume either way, that a failed sign-in cannot tell us on its own, and draws only the sound conclusion (a successful sign-in proves the account exists).
- `isUncertainOutcome` is now an allowlist: only `AUTHORITATIVE_REJECTION_CODES` (22023, 28000, 42501, 53400, 54000, 55000 - the errcodes the migrations raise - plus PostgREST PGRST301/302) prove "nothing was stored". ECONNRESET/ETIMEDOUT/ECONNREFUSED, 5xx/gateway codes, 57014/40001/08xxx/XX000, PGRST000/003, unknown codes, status-only 401/502 and no-code errors all stay uncertain. Tests: domain (positive/negative code tables, copy never says nothing happened, deletion copy), api (ECONNRESET, 504, XX000 now uncertain), accounts browser suite (ECONNRESET/XX000/SOMETHING_NEW unconfirmed, 53400 still a specific confirmed refusal; after a lost deletion response the user signs out, sign-in fails because of the network, and the page never asserts deletion). No automatic retries added; no backend/idempotency change.

**2. Integrated validation (`npm run test:e2e:integrated`, `scripts/e2e-integrated.mjs`, 47 checks, CI step added)** - sequential, one mock for the whole app: end-to-end journey (find nearest, filter, open, back, Favorites gate, sign in, favorite, rate, settings, sign out), axe-core audit of 35 screen/width combinations, clickability sweep of every control on 16 screens at 390, 1280, 640x360 (200% zoom equivalent) and 320x180 (400% equivalent) to prove the sticky tab bar hides nothing, tab-label truncation, Tab sweep with visible-focus check, Enter + focus after navigation, cached-detail offline and recoverable errors, map controls, privacy/auth boundaries. Results: 47 passed, 0 failed (two consecutive runs); axe-core 0 violations on all 35 audits (report: `docs/evidence/r4/axe-report.json`). axe-core is the copy already in node_modules (transitive dev dependency); package.json untouched.

**Defects the integrated run demonstrated, and fixes (all milestone-scope):**
1. Going back from a restroom reset the filters and List|Map choice -> kept for the session (`state/discoverySession.ts`).
2. Result cards could be Tab-focused but not opened with Enter (react-native-web does not activate `role=link` Pressables) -> `enterActivates` on cards and the verified hint. This was an R1-era defect no earlier suite had caught (they clicked).
3. After opening a restroom, focus stayed on the hidden list -> pushed screens (detail, report, contribute, sign-in, reset, callback) move focus to the page heading; tab roots do not.
4. No visible keyboard focus on tab bar links and text inputs -> global focus-visible rule + `TextField` ring.
5. Map zoom controls were 30 px with no ring -> 44 px, focus ring, Tab-reachable (Leaflet's own keyboard support kept).
6. Tab labels truncated at 320 px ("Favo...") -> side padding removed, 13 px label, label overflow visible.
7. axe: content outside landmarks and heading order -> `main` landmark per screen, tab bar labeled "Main" navigation, card headings level 2.
Test-quality notes: my first focus-ring test raced React's focus-state render and my first tab-truncation test measured the wrong thing; both fixed. **Mutation check:** removing Enter activation, focus management, session filters or the global styles fails 10 checks (37 passed / 10 failed), restored afterwards.

**3. Other suites fresh and sequential after the changes:** `npm run check` exit 0 (lint, typecheck, domain 120, mobile 33, ui 28, importer/admin suites, secret scan); accounts 98; detail 83; discovery 75; auth and smoke pass. Android and iOS `expo export` both produce Hermes bundles (4.3 MB / 4 MB).

**4. Leaflet asset warnings inspected:** the export warns that `leaflet.css` (lines 359-407) imports local images (layers-control sprite, default marker icon path). The app uses neither (vector circle markers, no layers control), the browser run shows no broken request and the only third-party request is the configured tile provider (z/x/y paths only), so no runtime effect; left as is. Provider/legal gate unchanged.

**5. Visual inspection (screens viewed, not just generated):** phone discovery results, 320x180 detail (found and fixed the truncated tab labels and a stray heading focus box), wide discovery with map (zoom controls and ring), R3 deletion cleanup state, plus the earlier R1-R3 sets regenerated after the changes. The sticky tab bar is part of the layout (content scrolls above it), not an overlay; full-page captures show it overlapping only because Playwright stitches viewports.

**6. Coverage matrix, gaps and definition-of-done:** `REDESIGN_DOD.md` (new). Covered: Chromium (Playwright headless 1194) on Linux at phone, wide, 200% and 400% zoom equivalents. NOT covered, with severity and minimum owner action: Firefox and WebKit/Safari (not installed here; medium; run the suites in those browsers or approve a runner), native iOS/Android runtime (none available; native bundles compile only; high for launch), screen readers VoiceOver/TalkBack/NVDA (none; axe + DOM checks are a substitute, not equivalent; high for launch), physical touch/GPS (medium), text-only browser zoom/OS font scaling (web text is px-based so "text size only" settings are not honored on web; medium; possible follow-up), manual contrast review of tile imagery (low). **2. Evidence reconciliation (item 2) and my error.** Root cause: the mutation check ran the integrated suite on a deliberately broken build after the final run; the suite writes screenshots to docs/evidence, so the committed zoom400 image was the mutant's. I had claimed full labels from the passing run while shipping that image. Fix: every suite re-run from the final source in one clean sequential pass (discovery 75, detail 83, accounts 98, integrated 58, auth, smoke, `npm run check` exit 0), all of docs/evidence regenerated, and the 320x180 detail, phone results and map screenshots viewed (labels `Nearby Favorites Account Settings` whole; the stray heading focus box is gone). **3. No-cost browser and accessibility verification (item 3) - executed.** Playwright's CDN is blocked here, but Ubuntu's archive, conda-forge and npm are reachable, so I installed free tooling in the container/scratch dirs: Firefox 157.0.1 + geckodriver 0.37.1 (conda-forge), WebKitGTK 2.52.6 (`webkit2gtk-driver`, MiniBrowser) + xvfb (apt), selenium-webdriver 4.51.0 (npm, scratch), Orca 46.1 + AT-SPI 2.52 (apt). New optional scripts (not CI, not repo dependencies): `scripts/xbrowser-check.mjs`, `scripts/xbrowser-atspi.sh`, `scripts/atspi-dump.py`; full output with versions in `docs/evidence/r4/xbrowser-results.txt`.
- **Firefox 157 (28 checks, pass):** render/locate/results, Enter opens a card, focus moves to the heading, visible focus on every Tab stop, radio arrow keys with one tab stop, labeled inputs, navigation landmark with aria-current; browser-applied scaling via `layout.css.devPixelsPerPx` 2 (reflows to 640 CSS px) and 4 (reflows to 500 CSS px; Firefox's minimum window width stops 320), no horizontal scroll, whole labels, no covered control; the browser's minimum-font-size setting at 24 px (no overflow, nothing covered).
- **WebKitGTK 2.52.6 (23 checks, pass):** same journey; this is the WebKit engine on Linux, NOT Safari; zoom is window-size emulation (smallest layout reachable 447 CSS px).
- **Firefox platform accessibility tree over AT-SPI (24 checks, pass):** level-1/2 headings, exactly one checked radio button per group, expanded disclosure button, navigation landmark with four links, no tab roles in the document, unknown fact exposed as "Not reported", labeled entry/password fields. This is what a screen reader consumes; it is NOT a screen-reader run.
- **Orca:** installed and launched once ("Screen reader on."), but I could not get it to follow page focus or speak page content (launch hung intermittently; WebDriver-driven focus produced no speech). No screen-reader pass is claimed; the attempt is recorded in REDESIGN_DOD.
- **Not executable here:** Android emulator (no /dev/kvm; dl.google.com blocked), iOS (needs macOS), Safari, real devices, VoiceOver/TalkBack/NVDA/JAWS.
**Defects the new browsers found (fixed):** (a) Firefox showed that after client-side navigation the previous screens remained Tab-reachable behind the active one (aria-hidden but focusable): `enableScreens(true)` on web now makes inactive screens `display:none` with state kept (new Chromium check added); (b) WebKit/Firefox showed WebDriver key events do not set `:focus-visible`, so state-driven rings are used for the navigation links; (c) my own first Firefox "zoom" check requested a 1280 px window at DPR 2, never reflowed and passed vacuously - it now asserts the reflow and the docs say exactly which sizes were reached. Also tried and removed: Chromium `--force-device-scale-factor` changes devicePixelRatio but not CSS layout width in headless, so it is not zoom.

**4. Doc reconciliation (item 4).** `REDESIGN_DOD.md` has the corrected checklist and a per-runtime matrix of executed / attempted / not-executable with severity and smallest owner action; TESTING, DESIGN_SYSTEM, PROJECT_STATE updated. Unperformed and stated as such: native runtime, any screen reader, Safari, physical devices; text-only/OS font scaling only partially (minimum font size; web text is px-based). ## Delivery: loopback probe compatibility (response to Work review d101254) — 2026-10-10 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW.** Accepting Work's diagnosis: Metro on the Mac listened on `[::1]` only while my helper probed `127.0.0.1`, so a healthy Metro was declared dead (my probe, not a device/sandbox/account problem). I could not see this: this Linux host has no IPv6 loopback (`EAFNOSUPPORT`) and `localhost` is 127.0.0.1 here.

**Fix (`scripts/qa-metro.mjs`):** `probeStatus` tries `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]` and returns the first base URL whose `/status` answers; `startMetro` uses it and then fetches the manifest and bundle through that same address (`metro.base`); the timeout message lists every address tried; `freePort` now requires the port to be free on both 127.0.0.1 and ::1 (where IPv6 exists). The self-test's "no Metro is listening after the refusal" check uses the same probe (so an IPv6-only Metro could no longer be missed there). Still localhost-only, no tunnel, free ports, redacted bounded diagnostics, group cleanup, canary-only dotenv test. The mock and browser check own their own sockets on numeric 127.0.0.1 and are unaffected.

**Tests (Linux, IPv4 only):** 49 always-on (was 45): a real IPv4-bound stand-in server is found and its manifest + 600 KB bundle fetched through the discovered address; nothing listening gives null; a probe where only `[::1]` answers (the Mac result) finds it after 127.0.0.1 is refused; `startMetro` accepts an IPv6-only `/status` answer (stub child, labeled as NOT a real ::1 Metro). Mutation: restricting the host list to 127.0.0.1 fails the IPv6 tests (2 failures). `--dotenv` 52/52 and `--metro` pass on Linux; `npm run check` exit 0.
**NOT verified:** a real Metro listening on ::1. The selftest prints an INFO line and skips that case where IPv6 loopback does not exist (it runs a real ::1 stand-in server automatically where it does). Please re-run `npm run qa:selftest -- --dotenv` and `-- --metro` on the Mac; the real ::1 confirmation is yours to read from that output. Bundling remains distinct from native runtime; no native/device/reader result is claimed.

## Delivery: bundle retrieval through the reachable origin (response to Work review ba612b6) — 2026-10-10 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW.** Accepting the finding: startup probing was fixed, but `bundle()` still fetched the manifest's advertised origin (`http://127.0.0.1:P/...`) even when Metro answered only on `::1`/`localhost`.

**Fix (`scripts/qa-metro.mjs`, `reachableUrl`):** the advertised launch-asset URL is re-pointed onto the successfully probed base, keeping path and query (the `\u0026` unescape still applies first). It is accepted only if it is http(s), the host is a loopback name (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`) AND the port equals this Metro's assigned port; anything else (foreign host, look-alike host, other port, `file:`, garbage) throws "refusing to fetch it" and is never requested. IPv4 behavior, free ports, redacted bounded diagnostics, the env guard and group/canary cleanup are unchanged.

**Tests (57 always-on, was 49):** a stand-in that answers on 127.0.0.1 but ADVERTISES `http://[::1]:P/...` (the Mac case mirrored, since this host has no IPv6) is fetched correctly through the base; path/query preserved; five refusal cases; a Metro advertising a foreign origin makes `bundle()` fail with the refusal. Where IPv6 exists the suite also runs a REAL `::1`-only stand-in that advertises a 127.0.0.1 launch-asset URL (what Work saw); here it prints INFO and skips. Mutation: fetching the advertised URL unmodified fails the new test. Linux results: `--dotenv` 60/60, `--metro` 66/66 (both Metro bundles), `npm run check` exit 0.
**NOT verified by me:** a real Metro on `::1` (Work's sequential Mac `--dotenv` then `--metro` runs are the confirmation). No native runtime, device or reader pass is claimed.

**Fix:** `refusal_probe` now runs through the harness's own clean child environment (`env -i`, the harness's temporary HOME) with explicit test-only values only (`PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` and `PGHOST` set to an unreachable socket path inside the harness-owned directory, never created: no TCP, no network, no credential-file fallback). Real-psql validation is kept (a refusal counts only when psql itself exits 2 with its own connection error) and so are the broken-helper controls (missing psql, exit-127 stub, exit-2 stub without a connection error never count).
**New isolation checks (7; isolation is now 22/22):** (1) a stub that records the environment it receives, run while PGSERVICE, PGSERVICEFILE, PGPASSFILE, PGPASSWORD, PGOPTIONS, PGDATABASE, PGUSER, PGPORT, PGSSLMODE, DATABASE_URL, SUPABASE_URL and HOME are all exported poisoned, proves none of them reach the probe, HOME is the harness's own, the only PG variables are PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT and PGHOST, and PGHOST is the explicit harness socket; (2) the REAL psql under an inherited poisoned PGSERVICE/PGSERVICEFILE still tried only the explicit socket (its own error names it and never the service's path); (3) a control shows an UNCLEAN psql run IS redirected by that same inherited service, so the check can really detect a leak. **Negative proof against the old behavior:** the previous probe line run through the same harness fails 4 of the new checks and exits 1 (`docs/evidence/rewards/p1-failclosed.txt`).
**Reruns on the final source (Claude's Linux PG16.13):** isolation 22/22; 10 test files all PASS, 306 ok lines, 0 failed; mutations 23 killed, 1 documented equivalent, 0 survived unexpectedly, 0 invalid; boundary 23/23 against `90a07e8` plus self-test 12/12; secret scan passed. SQL is unchanged since `68565c5`; only `scripts/test-db-rewards.sh` changed in the implementation (`0e69c5f`).
**Changed files since `68565c5`:** `scripts/test-db-rewards.sh`; `docs/evidence/rewards/` (README, `p1-test-run.txt`, `p1-mutations.txt`, `p1-boundary.txt`, `p1-failclosed.txt`); `PROJECT_STATE.md`, `BACKLOG.md`, `TESTING.md`, this file. All on the allowed list.
**Limits unchanged:** Work cannot run PG16 (OS-WORK-PG16-RUNTIME, Jake's call; no PG18 substitute, version guard untouched); these results are Claude-reported until Work reproduces them on approved PG16. Work can reproduce the stub-based isolation checks without PostgreSQL only if they stop before cluster start, which the harness does not do; I did not split that out (not requested). Waiting on Work's review.

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW.** Local, disposable PostgreSQL 16.13 only. NO production or hosted connection, NO migration applied anywhere, NOTHING moved into `supabase/migrations/`, no existing migration, `check_in`, app, package, CI or env file touched, no dependency, service or cost. Rewards stay DISABLED and the global budget UNSET; only test fixtures on the throwaway cluster enable them. P2-P4 not started. Handoff SHA is the commit carrying this entry.
**Allowed-file inventory (boundary check against `90a07e8`: 23/23):** new `supabase/rewards-wip/R001-R004*.sql`; `supabase/tests/rewards/` (helpers, 9 test files, `mutations.tsv`, `snapshot_fn.sql`, `golden_baseline_acl.sql`); `scripts/test-db-rewards.sh`; `scripts/check-rewards-boundary.mjs`; `docs/evidence/rewards/` (README plus three logs); edits only to `package.json` (one script, `test:db:rewards`), `PROJECT_STATE.md`, `BACKLOG.md`, `TESTING.md` and this file. No boundary exception was needed.
**Results (Claude's Linux container; Work has NOT reproduced them):** isolation self-test 11/11 with poisoned inherited variables and real server identity checks; 9 test files, 261 ok lines, 0 failed (`npm run test:db:rewards`); mutation checks 21 killed, 1 documented equivalent, 0 survived unexpectedly, each mutant built from a copy in its own throwaway cluster; boundary 19/19 plus 12/12 negative controls; `npm run check` exit 0 (lint with the one pre-existing warning, domain 132, mobile 65, ui 35, secret scan). Measured: 62,454 visits / 124,908 ledger rows, record_visit_at median 10.26 ms, p95 17.88 ms (this container only, not a capacity claim).
**Safety defaults:** fresh cluster `rewards_enabled=false`, budget NULL; disabled and budget-unset visits are recorded, keep their ordinal slot and issue nothing; a direct writer insert into the ledger is refused while disabled or unset; clients and the writer cannot change settings; `rewards-wip` contains no statement that enables rewards or sets a budget (the boundary check enforces it).
**Catalog (golden list in the evidence):** writer role NOLOGIN/NOINHERIT/no BYPASSRLS owning only `public.record_visit`, the internal time-injected `rewards.record_visit_at` and the deferred consistency trigger function; all tables owned by the migration role with RLS enabled AND forced and exactly 14 policies; anon, authenticated and service_role have no table, column, sequence or schema privilege and no EXECUTE except `authenticated` on `public.record_visit`; only four SECURITY DEFINER functions; every function has a fixed search_path; UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE refused for superuser, writer and every client role; golden diff of existing objects shows ONE change: USAGE on schema `auth` for the writer (needed for `auth.uid()`).
**Concurrency (real sessions through the public RPC as `authenticated`):** idempotency from two sessions, 4th-slot race, 60th-point race, user-cap race across two locations, last budget unit (forced spendable-block failure on a first visit keeps the lifetime award, no spendable row, no user counter), 20 users against 10 budget units (exactly 10/10, counter +50), 24 mixed-order two-visit transactions with no deadlock; REAL 55P03 caught by a named handler, REAL 57014 timeout and cancellation propagate and abort the whole visit.
**Findings during the build, for Work's attention:** (1) a deferred INVOKER trigger runs at commit as the session role, so the consistency trigger had to be SECURITY DEFINER or every client visit fails; superuser-run tests hid it, the two-session tests found it. (2) A first mutation run showed a test-helper defect (a probe's failure verdict was rolled back with the probe); fixed, mutant now killed. (3) TRUNCATE of FK-referenced tables is refused by PostgreSQL before any trigger, so the truncate guard is mutation-tested on the ledger.
**Decisions made within scope (please confirm or correct):** `public.locations` has no zone column, so `rewards.reward_location_zones` holds it and nothing populates it (every real visit would record the added outcome `no_valid_time_zone` until a later approved step); `rewards.location_check` is a narrow read-only SECURITY DEFINER helper owned by the migration role so the writer needs no access to `public.locations`; FKs are `on delete restrict`, so deleting a user or location with reward data is refused until the P2 purge exists (these files must not be promoted before P2); `profiles.points_balance` is not written (balances derived); the ledger has the reversal columns but a trigger refuses any reversal row; no per-user visit rate limit exists, as the plan specifies, so a spoofed-position client could add visit rows without bound until a later approved control; the one lock level (per-user/location/day) is redundant under the per-user lock and is the documented equivalent mutant.
**Not run / not claimed:** macOS, other PostgreSQL versions, hosted Supabase, any app or device, any production data, any P2-P4 behavior. No legal, privacy or security certification is claimed. Waiting on Work's independent review (`npm run test:db:rewards`, then `--mutations`).

## Delivery: D3 navigation correction (response to Work 382f1a7) — implementation `4ac0219`, evidence `79886f8` — 2026-10-12 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW.** Local only; no deployment, merge, migration, dependency, backend change, rewards work or cost. Dashboard composition and the other four D3 corrections unchanged.
**Defect confirmed and fixed.** Tab history de-duplicates routes and drops earlier params, and gives no native gesture. **Fix (existing Expo Router only, no new dependency):** root `_layout.tsx` is a `Stack`; the four main screens moved into the `(tabs)` route group (`src/app/(tabs)/`: index, favorites, account, settings; URLs unchanged) with their own `Tabs` layout (`backBehavior="history"` for tab presses); `location/[id]`, `contribute`, `report`, `auth/*` are pushed stack screens, so each push is its own history entry with its own route AND params and the native stack supplies the iOS edge-swipe and Android system Back. `PushedNav` (bottom Main bar under pushed screens, links PUSH) keeps the main destinations reachable; `BackBar` now re-reads `router.canGoBack()` on every root-navigation-state change (not only on focus), so a mounted screen never shows a stale Back; with no history (direct link, reload, browser Forward) the control stays the named "Nearby restrooms", never "Back". Sign-in success uses `dismissTo` so Back never returns to a stale sign-in/prompt; Nearby closes its filter dialog on blur (draft discarded) so it is never left over another screen. Search, filters and List|Map are in the session store and survive. Auth redirect context (`next`) unchanged.
**Required regression cases (all in the Chromium suites):** Home -> Details A -> Nearby (pushed) -> Details B -> Back -> Back returns Details A with its own id (and a third Back returns the first Nearby); Details A -> Favorites (pushed) -> Back returns Details A; Details -> Report -> sign-in -> back/recovery (Back from the report form after sign-in returns the restroom); reload and direct link show the named control; browser Forward; filter dialog closed when leaving Nearby; canGoBack change while mounted covered by the root-state re-read. Router and browser history only; the physical gestures are not exercised by these.
**Verification (sequential, final source, Chromium + local mocks):** `npm run check` passed (domain 132, mobile 65, lint 1 pre-existing warning); discovery e2e **156/156**, detail **89/89**, accounts **115/115**, integrated **58/58** (axe 0 serious/critical on 35 audits), auth and smoke passed; `npm run packet:d3` regenerated 17 screenshots. **Native:** `expo export --platform ios` and `--platform android` both completed (route config and bundles compile, Hermes bytecode produced); no native runtime, simulator or device is available here, so the physical iOS edge-swipe, Android hardware/gesture Back, VoiceOver/TalkBack and focus return on device are **NOT RUN** for the owner's device checks (`MOBILE_QA.md` section 9 updated with the A -> Nearby -> B -> Back -> Back case). Mutation check: making the pushed-nav links not push failed an earlier discovery check (the run stopped there, so the history block was not separately mutation-tested): stated plainly.
**Known limits:** a browser Forward rebuilds state from the URL (no history, so the named control shows); a pushed Nearby/Favorites over a restroom is a second mounted instance of that tab screen (state kept by the session store, data refetched). **Pinned review revision for Work to present:** `79886f8f8634b429f1a979ceba1a7fa2ebe13685` on claude-local with the existing QA mock setup.

## Acknowledgement and delivery: final G3 consistency corrections (Work 22423f3) — 2026-10-11 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW. Planning only; no code, SQL, schema, migration, dependency, UI, activation, scheduler, service, live change, deployment, merge or cost; no tests apply or were run, nothing implemented, no results fabricated.** Three inconsistencies fixed in `ACHIEVEMENTS_PLAN.md`:
1. **Metric/copy pairs (section 3):** Explorer of the Month and the seasonal example now use window-scoped `distinct_locations` ("5 different restrooms"); the award-count metric is renamed `qualifying_awards`, used only by explicitly visit-count rules and never described as places; First Stop is `qualifying_awards >= 1`; Regular/Century are points thresholds with points copy. Six worked expected-result cases: five days at one place (Explorer 1/5, Steady 5/8), five different places (5/5), a configurable point value (never "10 visits"), reversal of a non-last vs the last standing award at a place (no revoke vs revoke), reversal on a shared date.
2. **Lock order and bounded repair (section 5):** one universal order for every writer and repair: user advisory lock first, then that user's queue/chain rows. Repair selects candidates with a NON-locking read capped at `least(p_limit, 20)`, tries each user lock with `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` (skip on failure), then rechecks the queue row under the lock; per-user work capped at 50 chains per call with the remaining `dirty_scope` retained in the queue row (deleted only when empty). Clarified that evaluation runs INSIDE the visit/clearance/reversal transaction before commit, not as a post-commit hook. Expected outcomes for repair-versus-reversal interleaving and a 500-chain backlog included.
3. **Account-scoped deletion chains (section 6):** `achievement_chains(chain_id opaque random uuid, user_id, key, period)` with `unique (user_id, key, period)`; events keyed by `chain_id` with `unique (chain_id, seq)` and no user_id; closure keyed by chain_id and the caller's user_id, never key+period; retained chain = any revoke event, severed by `user_id` -> NULL with `chain_id` unchanged; minimized, explicitly linkable-not-anonymous retained fields; guard triggers require the purge-role check on every mutation including UPDATE-to-NULL (shape alone is not enough), DELETE only for chains without a revoke; tests: two users sharing key/period with one revoke, repeated deletion after severing, refused NULL updates by other roles and by the purge role on a revoke-less chain.
Remaining gaps (section 10): late-award prominence is a later content choice; single-progressbar device behavior unverified; the constants 20 users / 50 chains / SQLSTATE list are proposed for Work to confirm; everything is unimplemented and untested. Names, thresholds and dates remain proposals for later owner content review. Waiting on Work's acceptance review; D2 remains NOT ACCEPTED pending Jake's iPhone feedback.

## Acknowledgement and delivery: G2 planning corrections (Work 854fd47) — 2026-10-11 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW. Documentation/planning only; no ledger, schema, RPC, UI, migration, deployment, live change or cost; no tests apply or were run.** All four corrections made in `VIP_REWARDS_PLAN.md` (sections 3, 4a, 5, 7, 11.4, 11.4a, 11.7, 11.9); no owner policy re-opened.
1. **Independent outcomes vs rollback:** the award function now has two DISJOINT inner blocks, lifetime (L, guarded by a unique index on user/location/local day for lifetime awards, so exactly one even under races or after a reversal) and spendable (S, user then global counters plus the spendable row). A failed S rolls back only its own writes and never removes an eligible lifetime award; the whole visit stays all-or-nothing in one outer transaction (unexpected error = nothing recorded, retry with the same key). Budget unset/rewards disabled still issues nothing (gate runs first). Added the first-visit-global-exhaustion example, the user-cap race (55/60, two locations, exactly one spendable + two lifetime either order) and the double-submit example; test 5 rewritten.
2. **`award_held_visit` (new 11.4a):** uses only the visit's frozen original policy version, original local/UTC days and the ordinal locked at acceptance; never today's policy, never an interval, so no cooldown can apply or reset; L and S disjoint with appended immutable outcome events; a spendable cap failure at clearance is final and keeps the lifetime award; idempotent and race-safe clearance (unique clear decision per visit, row lock, per-kind uniqueness); no backdating (award `created_at` = clearance time, reconciliation by stored days); newer policy versions never apply to a held visit; independent admin, no self-adjudication. Tests added (item 9).
3. **No-cooldown rule enforced across the spec:** the "rapid repeat visits" signal is informational only and never a hold or refusal; implausible movement applies only between DIFFERENT locations; test 2 asserts four rapid visits produce no hold and that no code path computes a same-location interval. 4. **Deletion privacy/security (11.7):** minimum aggregate specified (`utc_month` + four amount totals + `visit_count`; no user/cohort/session/device/IP/location/time-of-day identifier), keyed by UTC MONTH instead of day as a minimization choice (reconciliation now checked per month), no client grants/readers, admin-gated monthly reporting only, low-count months acknowledged as residual risk, severed moderated rows keep only audit-needed fields (distance/accuracy buckets and idempotency key cleared), retention follows the reviewed Phase 3A rule (no number invented), and the text no longer calls severed detailed history anonymous or claims legal compliance.

## Delivery: G1 second-round technical corrections (Work 67f3dd2) — 2026-10-11 (Claude)

**Status: NEEDS_CHATGPT_REVIEW** (documentation only; no code, migration or test run). Done after D2 delivery as Work directed. All three points are in `VIP_REWARDS_PLAN.md`:
1. **No cross-row CHECK.** Only single-row CHECKs remain (amount sign versus `entry_type`, and which nullable columns must be set). Reversal matching is enforced by `reverse_reward(entry, decision)` (admin-gated security-definer function that locks the original row `FOR UPDATE`, verifies it exists and is an award, verifies the moderation decision exists, is a final reward-reversal decision and was not made by the user concerned, then copies user/visit/kind/version from the original and sets `amount = -original.amount`, with `on conflict (reverses_entry_id) do nothing` returning the existing row) plus a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger repeating the checks under the same lock. Concurrency tests listed: two simultaneous reversals give one row, reversal racing deletion is never half-applied, invalid reversal shapes and direct client inserts are refused.
2. **No GUC-authorized deletion.** The purge function is owned by a dedicated non-login role, EXECUTE is revoked from public/anon/authenticated, the trigger permits DELETE (and the narrow `user_id -> null` severing update for moderated rows) only when `current_user = 'reward_purge_owner'` (a security-definer owner cannot be set by a caller, unlike `set_config`), the user comes from `auth.uid()` via `delete_my_account` (never an argument), and moderated evidence is preserved unconditionally (severed, never deleted; moderation tables untouched). Authorization tests listed, including that a `set_config('os.account_deletion', 'on')` attempt is still denied.
3. **Atomic rollback and day basis.** The user-counter increment, global-counter increment and ledger inserts run in one inner PL/pgSQL block (an implicit savepoint): a failed global increment rolls the earlier increments back and the visit is recorded `recorded_only` / `budget_exhausted`, so budget is never consumed without an award. Day basis: per-location caps, cooldown and first-daily lifetime use the location-local day; the cross-location per-user cap and the global budget use one UTC day, with both days stored per visit. Counters measure GROSS issuance and reversals do NOT reopen them (they reduce outstanding balance only); issued/reversed/outstanding are reported per UTC day and the deleted-user aggregates store issued and reversed on the same basis, with the reconciliation identity written out. I did not change any approved reward value or rule. Two new owner decision rows (9: the UTC-versus-local basis for cross-location/global caps; 10: reversal versus issuance counters) were added to the batched table, and the G2 gate now lists them.

**Disposition: CORRECTIONS_REQUESTED for the disclosed progress-loop issue.** Implementationa66eba21568a7bdc6f9e6460605dadc8f8bb2cd7; evidence/handoffb9f7bfba7cf9b2eeffde15b34a25cfacae3503f4; HANDOFF blob0e9410174b245e9db37d4e06d1b470f13fae35a7; exact Claude-section UTF-8 FNV-1a64 4965a695607bba.
